Maybe we can derive gradient for max coefficient and do subgradient. Let hot lags set H where c_k ≈ max. For each x_i (first half index i), derivative of c_k:
full a has indices: first half f_i=x_i (i=0..h-1); second half r_j = 2 - x_{h-1-j} at index h+j.
For a variable x_i, it appears in a_i and a_{2h-1-i}=a_{n-1-i}=2-x_i.
Convolution c_k = ∑ a_p a_{k-p}. Derivative w.r.t x_i:
From a_i: ∂ = a_{k-i} (if 0≤k-i<n)
From a_{n-1-i}: ∂ = a_{k-(n-1-i)} * ∂a_{rev}/∂x_i = - a_{k-n+1+i} (if valid).
So ∂c_k/∂x_i = a_{k-i} - a_{k-n+1+i}. Then subgradient of max over H: sum or average over hot k. We can take gradient g_i = ∑_{k∈H} (a_{k-i} - a_{k-n+1+i}). Then move x_i -= lr * g_i (with constraints [0,2]) and maybe normalize? Since sum fixed. We can compute hot set as top few coefficients. This is a subgradient descent for minimizing maximum of quadratics, nonconvex but may help. We can implement with FFT? The gradient computation for hot set can be O(|H|*n) if many; but H small (top 1-50). We can just for each hot k compute contributions vector via slicing? For each k, a_{k-i} for i range is a slice; a_{k-n+1+i} slice. We can accumulate gradient array length h.

Pseudo:
```
def subgrad_step(a, lr, nhot=5, smoothing=0):
    n=len(a); h=n//2
    c = conv_fft(a)
    # find top nhot indices maybe with tie tie including symmetric?
    top_idx = np.argsort(c)[-nhot:]
    g = np.zeros(h)
    for k in top_idx:
        # i such that 0 <= k-i < n -> i in [max(0,k-n+1), min(h-1,k)]
        i_lo = max(0, k-n+1)
        i_hi = min(h-1, k)
        if i_hi >= i_lo:
            g[i_lo:i_hi+1] += a[k - i_hi : k - i_lo -1 : -1]? Need indices: for i from i_lo..i_hi, k-i decreasing from k-i_lo to k-i_hi. Use np.take.
        # rev term: index j = k - n + 1 + i; need 0 <= j < n. i_lo2=max(0,n-1-k), i_hi2=min(h-1, 2n-2-k?) because j<=n-1 -> i <= n-1-k? Wait j=k-n+1+i, require 0<=j<n -> n-1-k <= i <= 2n-2-k. Intersect 0..h-1.
        v_lo=max(0, n-1-k)
        v_hi=min(h-1, 2*n-2-k)
        if v_hi>=v_lo:
            g[v_lo:v_hi+1] -= a[v_lo: v_hi+1]? For i in v_lo..v_hi, j=k-n+1+i increasing from ... to ..., so a[j] = a[v_lo - (n-1-k)? Actually j = k-n+1+i. Let base = k-n+1+v_lo. Then indices base..base+(v_hi-v_lo). Use slice.
    # x_i are first half; move a and rev accordingly.
    new_left = np.clip(a[:h] - lr*g, 0,2)
    # build new a
```

Let's design a robust multi-phase optimizer:

Phase A: Fast internal FFT optimization over anti-palindromic block sequences for several lengths and block counts.
Phase B: Full anti-palindromic local search with subgradient and random moves.
Phase C: Optional general (non-pair) local search with normalization, maybe start from best anti and allow asymmetry.
Phase D: Time guard and return best evaluated by evaluate_sequence.

Let's detail internal functions:

`def _convolution_full(a, size=None):`
    n=a.size
    m=2*n-1
    if size is None:
        size=1
        while size < m: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, size=size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size=size)[:m]
    # To avoid small negatives, maybe c = np.maximum(c,0)
    return c

`def _internal_score(a):`
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    c=_conv(a)
    return float(2.0*n*c.max()/(s*s))

Since we fix sum=n for anti-pal, J=2*max/n. But for general use sum.

`def _evaluator_safe(a):` calls evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()), catches? It's imported.

`def _make_anti(n, left):`
    h=n//2
    left = np.clip(np.asarray(left, dtype=float), 0.0, 2.0)
    a = np.empty(n)
    a[:h]=left
    a[h:]=2.0 - left[::-1]
    return a

Need handle odd? We'll use even.

`def _block_anti(n, block_vals, repeats):`
    B=len(block_vals); h=B*repeats
    left=np.repeat(block_vals, repeats)
    a half? If n=2*h. Return _make_anti(n,left)

But if left is block repeated, right reverse complement gives block pairs aligned? Yes. If left blocks [v0,...,v_{B-1}] repeated L; left[::-1] gives reverse of all elements, which corresponds to first half blocks reversed at element level, not just block level if L same: right blocks correspond to 2 - v_{B-1},...,2-v0 each repeated L. Good.

Maybe simpler write specific local search for block anti:
```
def _local_search_blocks(block_vals, n, repeat, time_limit, rng, verbose=False):
    best_vals = block_vals.copy()
    best_a = _make_anti(n, np.repeat(best_vals, repeat))
    best_score = _internal_score(best_a)
    step = max(0.05, 0.5 / max(1,len(best_vals)**0.5))
    no_improve=0
    while time ...:
        # choose move type
        if rng.random()<0.5: coordinate
           idx=rng.integers(m)
           delta=rng.normal(0, step)
           new = clip(best_vals[idx]+delta,0,2)
           vals = best_vals.copy(); vals[idx]=new
        else: block of k coords
           k=rng.integers(1, max(2,m//4))
           idxs=rng.choice(m,k,replace=False)
           vals=best_vals.copy()
           vals[idxs]+=rng.normal(0,step,idxs); clip
        a=_block_anti_from_vals(vals)
        score=_internal_score(a)
        if score < best_score*(1-1e-9):
             best...; no=0
        else no+=1
           if no>100: step*=0.9; if too small reset; or occasional big jump
```
But this is slow if building/converting each time. m small, OK. Use internal FFT each eval. For m=32,n=2048, each eval ~ maybe 0.001-0.003. 100k evals ~300s. Good.

Let's design a custom block local search that can run with time control and is deterministic-ish.

`_local_search_blocks(vals, B, repeat, deadline, rng, improve_factor=1e-9):`
- best_vals = copy; best_a = build; best_score = internal.
- `step = 0.25` (values range 2).
- `no=0`
- while time < deadline:
   - Move selection:
     * 0.35 coordinate: idx random, new = best[idx] + rng.normal(0, step)
     * 0.35 small block: length k random 1..max(2,B//5), idxs random without replacement, add normal*step
     * 0.15 big block contiguous: start, width random 1..B//3, add normal*step
     * 0.15 global scale/mirror: add normal*step to all? Or random reset one quadrant.
     * 0.10 copy best with large jump: sample new from uniform [0,2] for a fraction or all? Maybe if no_improve>500.
   - Build and score. If score < best_score*(1 - 1e-10): accept, no=0.
     else no++; if no % 100==0: step *=0.95; if step<0.01: step=0.3; perform random restart from best with a large perturbation? Or generate fresh start and if better after local? We can just continue with big jumps.
- Return best.

`_full_anti_local_search(a, deadline, rng, eval_true_every=1000):`
- n=len(a), h=n//2.
- best_left=a[:h].copy(); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=_internal_score(best_a); best_true maybe from consider.
- Track time, no_improve.
- Learning rates.
- Move types:
  1. Subgradient top-hot (30%)
  2. Block Gaussian perturb on left (30%): choose start, width 1..max(2,n//64) or n//16? Since n=2048, width 8-64. Add gauss*sigma. Sigma adaptive (0.05-0.5).
  3. Random coordinate(s) (20%): width 1-4.
  4. "Anti-pair rescale" (10%): multiply a block by exp(gauss(0,sigma)) but clip [0,2] and set rev complement? If we modify left only, rev auto complement. Multiplicative may violate complement? We always rebuild: left new, right=2-left_rev. So any left modification works in [0,2]. Multiplicative around 1.
  5. Occasional large restart (10% if no improve): take best_left, apply strong random block changes or resample fraction.
- After each candidate, internal score. If improved, accept. If internal < best_true? We can periodically call evaluate to update true.
  But calling evaluate for every improvement may be many (could be thousands) but n=2048 okay. To save, call true every, say, 100 internal improvements or every 1s. Also at end.
- Adaptive step: if no_improve > 100, sigma *=0.7; if >1000, try a large jump.

Need implement subgradient candidate carefully.

`_subgrad_candidate(left, n, K=8, rng=None):`
   h=n//2
   left=left.copy()
   a = np.empty(n); a[:h]=left; a[h:]=2-left[::-1]
   c=_conv(a)
   maxval=c.max()
   # If all small? no
   # choose hot indices: top K by c. To diversify, for each candidate maybe with prob choose top K plus random coefficients among top 100.
   top = np.argsort(c)[-int(K):]
   if rng is not None and c.size > K:
       # maybe include one random from top 10%
       extra = rng.integers(0, max(1, int(0.1*c.size)))
       if extra not in top: top=np.append(top, extra)
   g=np.zeros(h)
   for k in top:
      # positive term
      lo=max(0, k-(n-1)); hi=min(h-1, k)
      if hi>=lo:
          jmax=k-lo; jmin=k-hi
          g[lo:hi+1] += a[jmax:jmin-1:-1]
          if (k % 2)==0:
             i=k//2
             if lo<=i<=hi: g[i]+=a[i]
      lo2=max(0, n-1-k); hi2=min(h-1, 2*n-2-k)
      if hi2>=lo2:
          jmin2=k-n+1+lo2; jmax2=k-n+1+hi2
          g[lo2:hi2+1] -= a[jmin2:jmax2+1]
          # diagonal rev: 2*n-2-k even always? n maybe even -> 2n-2 even, minus k parity? Use if (2*n-2-k)%2==0
          if ((2*n-2-k) % 2)==0:
             i=(2*n-2-k)//2
             if lo2<=i<=hi2:
                r=n-1-i
                # Need ensure r in [h, n-1]? since i in first half, r in second half. If diagonal r==j. Subtract extra a[r]
                g[i] -= a[r]
   # normalize
   gmax=np.max(np.abs(g))
   if gmax == 0: return left
   lr = 0.05 / gmax  # max change 0.05
   new_left = left - lr*g
   new_left = np.clip(new_left, 0.0, 2.0)
   # If no change, add tiny random
   if np.allclose(new_left, left):
       new_left = left + rng.normal(0,0.01,size=h)
       new_left = np.clip(new_left,0,2)
   return new_left
```
But note left values are in [0,2] and sum of pairs 2; OK.
This subgradient step reduces hot conv. lr 0.05 maybe small; we can try multiple step factors if no improve.

`_make_anti_from_left(left,n)`.

For block Gaussian moves:
```
def _block_perturb_left(left, rng, width_range, sigma):
    h=left.size
    new=left.copy()
    width=rng.integers(width_range[0], width_range[1]+1)
    start=rng.integers(0, h-width+1)
    new[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width)
    new=np.clip(new,0,2)
    return new
```

```
def _anti_hill_climb(a, deadline, rng, check_every=200):
    n=a.size; h=n//2
    left=a[:h].copy()
    best_left=left.copy(); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=_internal_score(best_a)
    sigma=0.15
    no=0
    check_timer=start
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it+=1
       move_type=rng.random()
       if move_type<0.35:
          width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, h//32))) # 1 to 64 for 2048
       elif move_type<0.55:
          width = int(rng.integers(max(2,h//64), max(3,h//16))) # block
       else:
          width = 1
       start = rng.integers(0, h-width+1)
       candidate = best_left.copy()
       if move_type<0.25:
           # subgrad
           newleft = _subgrad_candidate(best_left, n, K=8, rng=rng)
           candidate = np.clip(newleft,0,2)
           score=_internal_score(_make_anti_from_left(candidate,n))
       else:
           # sample values. For coordinate, try a set of deltas and midpoints.
           candidates = []
           if width==1:
              idx=start
              base=best_left[idx]
              values = [base, 0.0, 2.0, 1.0]
              values += [base + rng.normal(0,sigma) for _ in range(4)]
              values = unique clip
              # evaluate each? That increases evals but coordinate improvement. For width1 maybe okay.
           else:
              # 4 random Gaussian perturbations
              for rep in range(4):
                  cand = best_left.copy()
                  deltas = rng.normal(0, sigma, width)
                  cand[start:start+width] = np.clip(cand[start:start+width]+deltas,0,2)
                  score=_internal_score(_make_anti(cand))
                  take best among reps
       # accept if best candidate score < best_score
```
This does multiple evals per iteration; internal fast. Good.

But for width1 trying 8 values * many iterations may be expensive but n=2048, still okay. Could do 4 values.

Helper functions:
- `_fft_convolve(a)`:
```
def _fft_convolve(a):
    n = a.shape[0]
    m = 2*n -1
    if m <= 0: return np.empty(0)
    size = 1
    while size < m: size <<=1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, n=size)[:m]
    # due to roundoff, c may have tiny negative; set max with np.maximum
    return c
```
Could cache size? Not necessary.

- `_internal_objective(a)`:
```
def _internal_objective(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    return float(2.0 * a.size * c.max() / (s*s))
```
If c.max() negative due to underflow? not.

- `_normalize_sum(a, target=None)`:
   if sum > 0 and target: return target*a/s; else return a.
Maybe for anti no.

def _anti_from_left(left):
    left = np.asarray(left, dtype=np.float64)
    h=left.size
    a=np.empty(2*h, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:h]=left
    a[h:]=2.0 - left[::-1]
    return np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
```

def _block_from_vars(v, repeat):
    v=np.clip(np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64),0,2)
    left=np.repeat(v, repeat)
    return _anti_from_left(left)
```

- `_local_search_blocks(v, repeat, deadline, rng)`:
   Use internal object. Since m small, we can do best-of-M each iteration.
```
def _local_search_blocks(v, repeat, deadline, rng):
    v = np.clip(np.asarray(v),0,2).copy()
    m=v.size
    best_v=v.copy(); best_a=_block_from_vars(v,repeat); best_s=_internal_objective(best_a)
    sigma=0.2
    no=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        improved=False
        # generate M candidates
        for _ in range(3):
           cand=best_v.copy()
           mode=rng.random()
           if mode<0.4:
              idx=rng.integers(m)
              cand[idx]=np.clip(cand[idx]+rng.normal(0,sigma),0,2)
           elif mode<0.7:
              w=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,m//4)))
              idxs=rng.choice(m,w,replace=False)
              cand[idxs]=np.clip(cand[idxs]+rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,2)
           elif mode<0.9:
              # contiguous
              w=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,m//3)))
              s=int(rng.integers(0,m-w+1))
              cand[s:s+w]=np.clip(cand[s:s+w]+rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,2)
           else:
              # coordinate extreme: pick a few set to 0/2/1
              idxs=rng.choice(m,min(3,m),replace=False)
              for idx in idxs:
                 cand[idx]=rng.choice([0.0,1.0,2.0,cand[idx]+rng.normal(0,sigma/2)])
                 cand[idx]=np.clip(cand[idx],0,2)
           a=_block_from_vars(cand,repeat)
           s=_internal_objective(a)
           if s < best_s*(1-1e-11):
              best_v=cand; best_a=a; best_s=s; improved=True; break
        if improved:
           no=0; sigma=min(0.8, sigma*1.05)
        else:
           no+=1
           if no%100==0:
              sigma*=0.85
              if sigma<0.02:
                 # big jump
                 jump_mask = rng.random(m)<0.5
                 best_v[jump_mask]=rng.uniform(0,2,jump_mask.sum()) if jump_mask.any() else best_v
                 sigma=0.3
    return best_v, best_a, best_s
```
Need if `jump_mask.sum()` zero; handle.

But the final `best_v` not expected. We can return a and score.

def _run_block_phase(deadline, rng, time_budget=None):
    if time_budget is None: time_budget = min(240, (deadline - time.monotonic())*0.2)
    phase_deadline = time.monotonic()+time_budget
    best_a=None; best_s=np.inf
    N=1024
    half_target=N//2
    for B in [2,4,8,16,32]:
       rem = phase_deadline - time.monotonic()
       if rem < 2: break
       repeat = max(1, half_target//B)
       # adjust N actual=2*B*repeat
       alloc = min(rem, time_budget/len(list) ... ) maybe each B get fraction: 0.2,0.2,0.25,0.25?
       # generate starts: 2 for B<=16, 3 for B=32
       for start_idx in range(nstarts):

           if use DE and start_idx==0:
              try DE...
           # local with deadline min(phase_deadline - 1, start_alloc)
           v,a,s = _local_search_blocks(v, repeat, local_deadline, rng)
           if s<best_s: best=a,s
    return best_a,best_s
```

Implementation details for anti hill climb:
```
def _anti_hill_climb(a, deadline, rng, min_time=1.0):
    n=a.size; h=n//2
    best_left=np.clip(a[:h],0,2).copy()
    # ensure a is anti? If not, project: best_left = (a[:h] + (2 - a[h:][::-1])? Wait if a not exact anti, choose left = a[:h]; right rebuilt. This may improve/worsen.
    best_a=_anti_from_left(best_left)
    best_s=_internal_objective(best_a)
    sigma=0.15
    no=0
    last_true = ... (not inside)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # generate candidates M=4
       improved=False
       for _ in range(4):
          mode = rng.random()
          cand = None
          if mode < 0.35:
             cand = _subgrad_candidate(best_left, n, K=8, rng=rng)
          else:
             cand = best_left.copy()
             if mode < 0.55:
                # small random coordinate(s)
                w = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, h//64)+1))
                start = int(rng.integers(0, h-w+1))
                cand[start:start+w] = np.clip(cand[start:start+w] + rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,2)
             elif mode < 0.75:
                # medium block
                w = int(rng.integers(max(2,h//128), max(3,h//32)))
                start = int(rng.integers(0, h-w+1))
                cand[start:start+w] = np.clip(cand[start:start+w] + rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,2)
             elif mode < 0.85:
                # coordinate extremes
                idx = int(rng.integers(h))
                newv = float(rng.choice([0.0, 2.0, 1.0, best_left[idx] + rng.normal(0,sigma)]))
                cand[idx]=np.clip(newv,0,2)
             elif mode < 0.95:
                # high-level local: one hot? Use subgrad with larger step? Another subgrad.
                cand = _subgrad_candidate(best_left, n, K=16, rng=rng)
             else:
                # large jump on fraction
                mask = rng.random(h) < 0.1
                if mask.any():
                   cand[mask] = np.clip(cand[mask] + rng.normal(0, 0.5, np.count_nonzero(mask)),0,2)
          a_cand = _anti_from_left(cand)
          s_cand = _internal_objective(a_cand)
          if s_cand < best_s*(1-1e-10):
             best_left = cand; best_a = a_cand; best_s = s_cand; improved=True; break
       if improved:
          no=0
          sigma = min(0.6, sigma*1.03)
       else:
          no += 1
          if no % 100 == 0:
             sigma *= 0.85
          if no > 1000:
             # large restart but keep best
             cand = best_left.copy()
             mask = rng.random(h) < 0.3
             if mask.any(): cand[mask] = rng.uniform(0,2,np.count_nonzero(mask))
             a_cand=_anti_from_left(cand); s_cand=_internal_objective(a_cand)
             # even if not immediate improvement, use as temp? Hill climb won't accept worse. Maybe just reset sigma.
             sigma=0.35; no=300
    return best_a, best_s
```
Hill climb only accepts improvements, so large worse jumps not used. To escape local minima, we might need accept temporary worse (simulated annealing) or keep multiple sequences. Our subgradient and random variations may accept better only; if stuck, no improvement forever. Need incorporate occasional non-improving moves or multiple basins.

Options:
- Keep a set of top K diverse sequences and iterate (parallel basin). When one stagnates, generate new from best with big perturbation and allow it to improve. But accepting worse from current prevents losing best. We can maintain `best` and `current` separate. If no improvement, we can update current to a perturbed version even if worse (annealing) while keeping best. But then search may wander. Use simulated annealing with temperature.

Candidate generation for anti phase detailed function `_anti_generate_candidate(current_left, n, sigma, rng)`:
```
def _anti_generate_candidate(left, n, sigma, rng):
    h=n//2
    r=rng.random()
    if r < 0.4:
        return _subgrad_candidate(left, n, K=int(rng.integers(4,17)), rng=rng)
    cand = left.copy()
    if r < 0.7:
        # width log-uniform 1..maxw
        maxw=max(1, h//16) # 256 for 4096? maybe too large; use h//64=64
        maxw = max(4, h//64)
        w = 1 + int(np.floor(np.exp(rng.uniform(0, np.log(maxw)))))
        w = min(w, h)
        s = rng.integers(0, h-w+1)
        cand[s:s+w] = np.clip(cand[s:s+w] + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, w), 0.0, 2.0)
    elif r < 0.85:
        w = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, h//256)+1)) # very local
        s = int(rng.integers(0, h-w+1))
        # choose value from uniform around base or extreme
        base = cand[s:s+w].copy()
        if rng.random()<0.5:
            base += rng.normal(0, sigma, w)
        else:
            base[:] = rng.uniform(0,2,w)
        cand[s:s+w]=np.clip(base,0,2)
    elif r < 0.95:
        # two mirrored blocks? maybe modify left and complement? Since right auto, okay.
        # flip a random block values around 1: cand[s:e] = 2 - cand[s:e]
        maxw=max(2,h//32)
        w=int(rng.integers(1,maxw+1)); s=int(rng.integers(0,h-w+1))
        cand[s:s+w]=2.0-cand[s:s+w]
    else:
        # global small perturbation
        cand = np.clip(left + rng.normal(0, sigma*0.5, h), 0,2)
    return cand
```
Width log-uniform needs avoid `np.log(0)`. Use maxw.

For width=1, `s:s+w` works.

SA acceptance with objective scale: `temp` initial maybe 0.005? Objective around 1.5. We want accept small worse. Since SA over many steps, use `temp=0.002` to 1e-4. If too low stuck; high wanders. We can adjust based on no_improve: if stall, temp increases? Basin hopping resets.

Maybe use deterministic hill climb plus periodic random restart from best with big jump rather than SA. But SA easier.

Let's design `_anti_sa(a, deadline, rng)`:
```
current = a[:h].copy(); best=current.copy()
current_s = _internal_distance(_anti(current)); best_s = current_s
sigma=0.12; temp=0.003; stall=0; improve_stall=0
while time < deadline:
    cand = _anti_generate_candidate(current,n,sigma,rng)
    s = _internal_objective(_anti_from_left(cand))
    if s < best_s:
       best=cand; best_s=s; improve_stall=0
    else: improve_stall+=1
    if s < current_s + temp:
       current=cand; current_s=s; stall=0
    else:
       stall+=1
    # adapt
    if stall>80:
       # reset current to best or perturbed best
       if rng.random()<0.7:
          current = best + 0? maybe exact
          current_s = best_s
       else:
          current = best.copy()
          mask = rng.random(h)<0.1
          current[mask]=rng.uniform(0,2,mask sum)
          current_s = _internal_objective(_anti(current))
       stall=0
       temp = max(0.0001, temp*0.5)
    if improve_stall>2000:
       sigma = max(0.02, sigma*0.7)
    else:
       sigma = min(0.5, sigma + 0.00005) # slow increase
    temp = max(1e-5, temp*0.9995)
    # print occasionally
```
But if current reset to best, candidate generation from best; good.

Maybe we should track best via internal score but if we do many SA accepted worse, current_s can be much worse than best; candidate improvements relative to current less meaningful. Resetting helps.

Alternative: Use "best-of-M hill climb" with temporary worst. Could be simpler and more reliable: At each iteration, start from best, generate several candidates, accept best improvement. If no improvement for long, generate a new random start and hill-climb it, keeping global best. This is basin hopping: We maintain a list of basins (currents). Each basin only gets better, so no SA temp. Periodically create new basin from best or random, and iterate over basins. Since objective may have many local minima, multiple basins help.

Implement parallel basins with maybe K=4 sequences. Each basin has left and score. Iteration: pick a basin (round-robin), generate candidate from it, if improves basin update it and global if needed. If a basin stagnates, either replace with a perturbation of global best or random. This avoids accepting worse into basin, so each basin monotonic. It may escape by creating new basins. Good.

Pseudo:
```
basins = [best_left.copy()]; scores=[best_s]
max_basins=6
it=0
while time:
   idx = it % len(basins)
   cand = generate(basins[idx])
   s=score(cand)
   if s < scores[idx]: basins[idx]=cand; scores[idx]=s; if s<global_best update
   else:
       stagnation[idx]+=1
       if stagnation[idx] > threshold:
           # replace basin
           if rng.random()<0.5 or not basins:
              new = large_perturb(global_best_left, sigma=0.3, fraction=0.1)
           else:
              new = random_left(rng)
           basins[idx]=np.clip(new,0,2); scores[idx]=score(new); stagnation[idx]=0
   it+=1
   # manage number of basins: if can improve, add copy of new basin? Keep max.
```
This may be better. Candidate generation can be hill-climb (improving) from basin. Since each basin only accepts improvements, it will converge to local. New basins explore.

We can also use a small SA within a basin? Not necessary.

How many basins? Internal evals fast, K=8 okay. Time overhead: replacing basin with worse is okay because global best separate.

Let's implement `_multi_basin_anti(initial_a, deadline, rng)`:
- h=n//2.
- Initialize basins:
   * best from initial anti.
   * a few random or periodic starts:
       - left from initial with large perturb (10-30%)
       - two-level high first half

General candidate generation details:
```
def _general_candidate(a, sigma, rng):
    n=a.size
    r=rng.random()
    cand=a.copy()
    if r<0.3:
       # anti projection
       h=n//2
       left=(cand[:h] - cand[n-1::-1] + 2.0)/2.0
       left=np.clip(left,0,2)
       cand=_anti_from_left(left)
    elif r<0.6:
       # multiplicative block
       maxw=max(4,n//64)
       w=1+int(np.floor(np.exp(rng.uniform(0,np.log(maxw)))))
       w=min(w,n)
       s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1))
       cand[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,w))
    elif r<0.8:
       # additive block
       maxw=max(4,n//64)
       w=...
       s=...
       mean = cand[s:s+w].mean() if w>0 else 1
       cand[s:s+w] += rng.normal(0, max(0.05, 0.1*mean), w)
    elif r<0.9:
       # coordinate: set a random small block to values sampled [0,2]
       w=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,n//256)))
       s=...
       cand[s:s+w]=rng.uniform(0,2,w)
    else:
       # mirror/complement components
       rev = cand[::-1]
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           cand = 0.5*(cand + rev)  # symmetrize
       else:
           cand = 0.5*(cand + (2.0 - rev))  # anti-ish? But complement maybe values not nonnegative if >2.
    # normalize
    cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
    ssum=cand.sum()
    if ssum <= 0:
       cand = np.ones(n); ssum=n
    cand *= n/summ
    cand = np.clip(cand,0,400)
    ssum=cand.sum()
    if ssum>0: cand *= n/summ
    return cand
```
But objective scale invariant, scaling to n okay. If some values >2 after scale, OK. Use cap 1000.

Final upsampling functions:
```
def _resample_array(a, n_target):
   if a.size == n_target: return a.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,a.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_target)
   b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
   return b

def _resample_left(l, h_target): similar
```

They described: Given f, solve LP maximizing sum b subject to b>=0 and ||f*g||∞ ≤ M, then rescale and update. This is a sophisticated method to reduce peak convolution. Can we implement it? Objective: We want minimize M = max conv(f) with normalization? They normalize sum(f)=sqrt(2n). In their formulation, LP: variables b (nonnegative), maximize sum b subject to f*b (convolution) infinity norm ≤ M, where M = current max conv(f). Then rescale b to normalization and update f = (1-t)f + t g. Since f*b ≤ M and b nonnegative, this tries to find a direction g that does not increase peak when added? Then rescale to same norm; repeated reduces M.

We can adapt with our objective. Let current a (nonnegative, sum maybe n). Let M = max conv(a). We want find b≥0 with conv(a,b)_k ≤ M for all k. Maximize sum b. Then rescale b to sum = sum(a) (or target) to get g. Then update a' = (1-t)a + t g. Since conv(a,g)≤M, but conv(a',a') may reduce? They claim nonincreasing. This is like Frank-Wolfe / Dantzig-Wolfe for minimizing max conv under sum constraint.

We can solve LP with cvxpy? Variables b length n. Constraints: conv(a,b) ≤ M. The convolution matrix is 2n-1 by n, dense O(n²). For n=1024, matrix 2047x1024 ~2M floats ~16MB, okay. We can construct with scipy.sparse or dense? cvxpy may handle. But direct matrix fill O(n²) in Python if using loops; use FFT? Convolution matrix lower/upper triangular. For fixed a, constraints c_k = ∑_{i} a_i b_{k-i}. This is a banded Toeplitz-like matrix times b. We can build sparse using scipy.sparse.diags? For each i, a_i contributes to row k ? If b_j, term in c_k when k-i=j -> k=i+j. So coefficient a_i at row i+j. For sparse, for each j, column j has a_i at rows i+j for i=0..n-1 => a full column. That's dense. Could build dense matrix C shape (2n-1,n): for each i, C[i : i+n, i] = a? Let's see C[k,j]=a_i where i+j=k => i=k-j. So column j has a_{k-j} for k=j..j+n-1. That is a reversed segment. We can fill in loop over j (n) and slice: `C[j:j+n, j] = a[:n]`? For column j, k from j to j+n-1, i=k-j 0..n-1, coefficient a_i. So C[j:j+n, j] = a. Yes. This can be done vectorized? For n=1024, loop 1024 slices, okay. Dense 2047x1024 ~2M, fill fast. For n=2048, 4M, 32MB, okay maybe. Constraints `C @ b <= M`.

Normalize function for general:
```
def _normalize_positive(a, target_sum=None):
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   if target_sum is None: target_sum=a.size
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return np.full_like(a, target_sum/a.size)
   a *= target_sum/s
   # if values too high, clip and renormalize
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s>0: a*=target_sum/s
   return a
```
If a has zeros all, fallback ones.

`_lp_direction`:
```
def _lp_direction(a, M):
    if not _HAVE_LINPROG: return None
    n=a.size
    if n > 1024: return None  # avoid huge
    C=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    # Fill using for j: C[j:j+n,j]=a
    # Use memory efficient: for j in range(n): C[j:j+n, j] = a
    for j in range(n):
        C[j:j+n, j] = a
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)]*n
    try:
       res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=C, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       if res.success and res.x is not None:
           x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
           x = np.clip(x,0,1000)
           if np.any(x>0): return x
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Could add upper bound to b to avoid numerical issues: `bounds=[(0.0, 10.0)]*n` maybe. Since normalized sum n, directions with values >10 not needed? Not guaranteed but likely. Upper bound can make bound but may restrict. Use None for robustness. HiGHS should handle.

But LP with `b_ub` list of floats, okay. If M is very small, b sum small.

Memory: For n=1024, C 2M, fine. Loop fill in Python maybe 1M; okay. For speed, can fill C using broadcasting? `C = np.zeros` then `C[np.arange(n)[:,None] ...]` too big. Loop fine.

Potential performance: `linprog` copies A; memory maybe 32MB. OK.

def _logunif_width(rng, minw, maxw):
    if maxw <= minw: return int(maxw)
    return int(min(maxw, 1 + np.floor(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(minw), np.log(maxw))))))

def _general_candidate(a, sigma, rng):
   n=a.size
   r=float(rng.random())
   if r < 0.25:
      # anti subgrad
      left = _project_left_from_full(a)
      left = _subgrad_candidate(left, n, K=int(rng.integers(4,17)), rng=rng)
      return _anti_from_left(left)
   cand = a.copy()
   if r < 0.6:
      maxw=max(4, n//64)
      w=_log_width(1,maxw,rng)
      s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1))
      # additive or multiplicative choose
      if rng.random()<0.5:
         cand[s:s+w] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.1*sigma? , w)
      else:
         cand[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, w))
   elif r < 0.8:
      # small coordinate
      w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//256)+1)); s...
      base=np.clip(cand[s:s+w],0,None)
      cand[s:s+w]=np.clip(base + rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,None)
   elif r < 0.9:
      # random values
      w=int(rng.integers(1, max(3,n//128)+1)); s...
      mean = cand[s:s+w].mean() if w else 1
      cand[s:s+w]=rng.uniform(0, max(0.1, mean), w)
   else:
      # sym/complement
      if rng.random()<0.5:
         cand = 0.5*(cand + cand[::-1])
      else:
         cand = 0.5*(a + (2.0 - a[::-1]))
   cand = _normalize_positive(cand, n)
   return cand
```

Let's design helper `_sanitize_sequence(a)`:
```
def _sanitize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    if a.size==0: return []
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 0.01:
       a = np.ones_like(a); s=a.sum()
    # keep sum maybe same, no need scale to fixed; but to avoid huge? scale to len?
    a = a / s * a.size
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s>0: a = a / s * a.size
    return a
```
Scale to length for consistency. Since objective invariant, okay. But if values clipped after scaling, renorm. Good.

When resampling, use `_sanitize_sequence`.

`_resample_array(a, n_target)`:
```
a=_sanitize_sequence(a)
if a.size==n_target: return a
x_old=np.linspace(0,1,a.size)
x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_target)
b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
return _sanitize_sequence(b)
```

`_project_left_from_full(a)`:
```
def _project_left_from_full(a):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    n=a.size; h=n//2
    if h==0: return np.array([])
    # if n odd, use floor? We use even.
    left = (a[:h] - a[n-1-h:n-1:-1]? Need a[n-1::-1][:h])
    rev = a[n-1::-1][:h]
    left = (a[:h] - rev + 2.0)/2.0
    left = np.clip(left,0,2)
    return left
```
Check for anti: a=[1,1,1,1] n=4 h=2 rev=[1,1], left=(1-1+2)/2=1. Good. For a=[2,0,0,2] (not anti? pair sums 4? n=4 pairs (0,3):2+2=4; formula left0=(2-2+2)/2=1, left1=(0-0+2)/2=1 -> anti all1). Projection.

But for general a normalized sum n, pair sums not 2; projection may change sum to n (anti build). Good.

Now, block starts. Let's write `_initial_block_vector(m, kind, rng)`:
- kind 0: constant 4/3.
- kind 1: half high half low: first m/2 = 1+0.3, second=1-0.3.
- kind 2: sinusoidal: x=np.linspace(0,1,m); 1 + A*sin(2π x + phase), A=rng uniform 0.3,0.9, phase uniform 0,2π. clip.
- kind 3: random uniform [0.2,1.8] or [0,2].
- kind 4: alternating: 1 + A*(-1)^i, A=0.25-0.75.
- kind 5: linear ramp: 1 + A*(x-0.5)*2, A=0.4-0.9, but ensure left values maybe high one side.
Return np.clip.

- basin_scores = [...]
- stagnation = [0]*len(basins)
- `best_print_time = time.monotonic() + 10`
- loop while time<deadline:
   choose idx: if rng.random()<0.5: idx = argmin(basin_scores) else rng.integers(len)
   cand = _block_candidate(basins[idx], rng)
   a=_block_from_vars(cand,repeat); s=_internal_objective(a)
   if s < basin_scores[idx]*(1-1e-11):
      basins[idx]=cand; basin_scores[idx]=s
      if s < global_score:
         global_best_v=cand.copy(); global_score=s
   else:
      stagnation[idx]+=1
      if stagnation[idx] > threshold:
         # replace
         choice=rng.random()
         if choice<0.5:
            new = _perturb_block_vector(global_best_v, rng, fraction=rng.uniform(0.05,0.3), sigma=0.3)
         elif choice<0.7:
            new = _perturb_block_vector(basins[idx], rng, fraction=0.5, sigma=0.5)
         else:
            new = _initial_block_vector(B, int(rng.integers(0,6)), rng)
         new=np.clip(new,0,2)
         a_new=_block_from_vars(new,repeat); s_new=_internal_objective(a_new)
         basins[idx]=new; basin_scores[idx]=s_new; stagnation[idx]=0
         if s_new < global_score: update
   # print every 20s
Return global_best_v, _block_from_vars(global_best_v,repeat), global_score

`_block_candidate(v, rng)`:
```
m=v.size
r=rng.random()
cand=v.copy()
if r<0.5:
  idx=int(rng.integers(m))
  cand[idx]=np.clip(cand[idx]+rng.normal(0,0.2),0,2)
elif r<0.8:
  w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,m//3)+1))
  idxs=np.sort(rng.choice(m,w,replace=False))
  cand[idxs]=np.clip(cand[idxs]+rng.normal(0,0.2,w),0,2)
else:
  # pick one or two extremes
  idxs=rng.choice(m, min(2,m), replace=False)
  for idx in idxs:
     val=float(rng.choice([0.0,1.0,2.0, 1.0+rng.normal(0,0.3)]))
     cand[idx]=np.clip(val,0,2)
return cand
```

Full basins selection and replacement:

def _full_basins(initial_sequences, deadline, rng, max_basins=6, threshold=400):
   # all sequences same length? resample to length of first (best? maybe choose median)
   n = initial_sequences[0].size
   inits=[]
   for s in initial_sequences:
      t=_resample_array(s,n); t=_sanitize_sequence(t,n); inits.append(t)
   scores=[_internal_objective(s) for s in inits]
   order=np.argsort(scores)
   global_best=inits[order[0]].copy(); global_score=scores[order[0]]
   basins=[inits[i].copy() for i in order[:max_basins]]
   basin_scores=[scores[i] for i in order[:max_basins]]
   stagnation=[0]*len(basins)
   sigma_base=0.08
   loop...
      idx pick best/uniform
      cand = _full_candidate(basins[idx], sigma_base, rng)
      s = _internal_objective(cand)
      if s < basin_scores[idx]:
         basins[idx]=cand; basin_scores=s
         if s < global_score:
            global_best=cand; global_score=s
            # if len<max, add perturbed copy
      else:
         stall++
         if stall>threshold:
             # replace with perturbed global or random
             fraction = rng.uniform(0.02,0.25)
             if rng.random()<0.4:
                new = _perturb_full(global_best, rng, fraction, 0.25)
             elif rng.random()<0.3:
                new = _perturb_full(basins[idx], rng, 0.5, 0.4)
             else:
                new = _random_general_array(n, rng)
             s_new=...
             basins[idx]=new; basin_scores=s_new; stagnation=0
             if s_new<global: update
      print...
   return global_best, global_score
```
`_perturb_full(a, rng, fraction, sigma)`: copy, mask random fraction, apply multiplicative or additive noise; normalize.
```
def _perturb_full(a, rng, fraction, sigma):
   a=a.copy(); n=a.size; mask=rng.random(n)<fraction
   if mask.any():
      if rng.random()<0.5:
         a[mask] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,mask.sum()))
      else:
         a[mask] += rng.normal(0,0.1*sigma,mask.sum())
   return _normalize_positive(a,n)
```

def _full_candidate(a, sigma, rng):
   n=a.size; r=float(rng.random())
   if r < 0.30:
       left = _project_left_from_full(a)
       # maybe perturb left then subgrad
       left = _subgrad_candidate(left, n, K=int(rng.integers(4,17)), rng=rng)
       return _anti_from_left(left)
   if r < 0.55:
       cand=a.copy()
       maxw=max(4, n//64)
       w=_log_width(1, maxw, rng)
       s=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       if rng.random()<0.5:
          cand[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, w))
       else:
          cand[s:s+w] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.1*sigma? no, 0.1, w)
       return _normalize_positive(cand,n)
   if r < 0.75:
       cand=a.copy()
       w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//256)+1))
       s=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       base=np.clip(cand[s:s+w],0,None)
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           cand[s:s+w]=np.clip(base + rng.normal(0,sigma,w),0,None)
       else:
           # set to sampled around current or 1
           mean = base.mean() if base.size else 1
           cand[s:s+w]=np.clip(rng.normal(mean,0.2,w),0,None)
       return _normalize_positive(cand,n)
   if r < 0.9:
       cand=a.copy()
       # random block values from uniform [0, max(0.5,mean)]
       w=int(rng.integers(1, max(3,n//128)+1)); s=...
       mean = cand[s:s+w].mean() if w>0 else 1.0
       cand[s:s+w]=rng.uniform(0.0, max(0.2, mean), w)
       return _normalize_positive(cand,n)
   else:
       # symmetrize/anti
       if rng.random()<0.5:
           cand=0.5*(a + a[::-1])
       else:
           left=_project_left_from_full(a)
           left=np.clip(left + rng.normal(0,0.1*a.size? no,0.1,left.shape),0,2)
           cand=_anti_from_left(left)
       return _normalize_positive(cand,n)
```

```
g=np.zeros(h)
for k in top:
  lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(h-1,k)
  if hi>=lo: g[lo:hi+1] += a[k-lo:k-hi-1:-1]
  lo2=max(0,n-1-k); hi2=min(h-1,2*n-2-k)
  if hi2>=lo2:
     jmin=k-n+1+lo2; jmax=k-n+1+hi2
     g[lo2:hi2+1] -= a[jmin:jmax+1]
g *= 2.0
lr = scale / (np.max(np.abs(g))+eps)
new_left = left - lr*g # since want reduce c; gradient of c wrt left. If g positive, decrease left.

Let's write LP loop with `M_factor` and `t_candidates`:
```
def _lp_optimize_sequence(a, deadline, rng):
    a = _sanitize_sequence(a, a.size)
    n=a.size
    best_a=a.copy(); best_s=_internal_objective(a)
    a=best_a.copy()
    no_improve=0
    M_factor=1.0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       c=_fft_convolve(a); M=float(c.max())
       if not finite or M<=0: break
       M_lp = M * M_factor
       b = _lp_direction(a, M_lp)
       if b is None:
           if M_factor > 0.7: M_factor -= 0.05
           else: break
           continue
       sum_b=np.sum(b); if sum_b<=0: break
       g = b * (a.sum()/sum_b)
       g = _sanitize_sequence(g, n) # ensure nonnegative sum n
       # try t
       improved=False
       for t in (0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01):
           cand=(1-t)*a + t*g
           cand=_sanitize_sequence(cand,n)
           s=_internal_objective(cand)
           if s < best_s:
              best_a=cand.copy(); best_s=s; a=cand.copy(); improved=True; break? Could continue to find even smaller t? We can check all and choose best.
       # choose best among t full
       ...
       if not improved:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>3: M_factor=max(0.7,M_factor-0.05)
          if no_improve>15: break
       else:
          no_improve=0; M_factor=min(1.0, M_factor+0.02?) maybe keep.
       if best_s < 1.5001: # target maybe, can continue but break soon
       # print
    return best_a,best_s
```
Rather than break at first t, evaluate all t and choose best. But that multiplies evals by 5; n=512 cheap. Let's do:
```
best_cand=a; best_t_s=best_s
for t in [0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02]:
   cand...
   s...
   if s<best_t_s: best_t_s=s; best_cand=cand
if best_t_s < best_s - 1e-10: update; improved=True
```
Good.

LP `M_factor` lowering: If no improve 2 times, lower to 0.95, then 0.9, 0.8. If still no improve, break. Could also increase to 1.1? Not.

Potential issue: If LP returns b very small (sum_b near 0), g normalized has high variance; update may be bad. We can require sum_b > 1e-6*n? Maybe if sum_b too small, skip. Use `if sum_b < 1e-6 * n: break`? Normalized direction sum n; b sum small due to M small but scaling makes g sum n. If b all zeros, fail. If sum_b extremely small, numerical. Use threshold 1e-8.

Now, `_normalize_positive(a, target_sum)` if a has tiny negative due to LP/solve, clip. It scales to target_sum. For LP, target_sum n.

Potential issue: In LP, if M_lp is too low, b sum maybe 0? b=0 feasible; LP maximizes sum, may still small. OK.

Let's think about using direct `np.convolve` in LP? No, FFT.

Now, target upper bound 1.5027. Our code hopefully. If not, maybe lower bound? But let's proceed.

`_fft_size` cache:
```
_fft_cache = {}
def _fft_size(m):
    key=m
    if key in _fft_cache: return _fft_cache[key]
    size=1
    while size < m: size <<=1
    _fft_cache[key]=size
    return size
```

`_internal_objective`: If a size very large and FFT too large? We cap? For internal candidate maybe n up to 10000; FFT size 16384. OK. If n > 20000, maybe heavy but not. Use if not finite return inf.

`_normalize_positive(a, target_sum=None)`:
```
def _normalize_positive(a, target_sum=None):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    if a.size==0: return a
    if target_sum is None: target_sum=float(a.size)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
       a=np.full_like(a, target_sum/max(1,a.size))
       s=float(a.sum())
    a *= target_sum / s
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s > 0:
       a *= target_sum / s
    else: a=np.full_like(a,target_sum/a.size)
    return a
```
If a.size not int? OK.

`_sanitize_sequence(a,target_sum=None)` same but returns float array. Could just use normalize. For final return, normalize to target length maybe.

`_anti_from_left(left)`:
```
left=np.asarray(left,dtype=float)
left=np.where(np.isfinite(left),left,0.0)
left=np.clip(left,0.0,2.0)
h=left.size
a=np.empty(2*h,dtype=float)
a[:h]=left
a[h:]=2.0-left[::-1]
return np.clip(a,0,1000)
```
If h=0, returns empty. But need n>0. Ensure not.

`_project_left_from_full(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
n=a.size; h=n//2
if h==0: return np.array([],dtype=float)
rev = a[n-1::-1][:h]
left = (a[:h] - rev + 2.0) / 2.0
return np.clip(left,0,2)
```
Check if n odd? We use even. If odd, h floor; rev length h. OK.

`_block_from_vars(v, repeat)`:

v=np.asarray(v,dtype=float)

v=np.clip(v,0,2)
left=np.repeat(v, int(repeat))
return _anti_from_left(left)
```
If repeat <=0: return _anti_from_left(v)? Use max(1).

`_block_internal(v, repeat)`: return _internal_objective(_block_from_vars(v,repeat)). Need for linprog? Not.

`_initial_block_vector(m, kind, rng)`:
```
def _initial_block_vector(m, kind, rng):
    m=int(m)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m) if m>1 else np.array([0.5])
    if kind==0:
        v=np.full(m,4.0/3.0)
    elif kind==1:
        v=np.ones(m); half=m//2; v[:half]=1.3; v[half:]=0.7
    elif kind==2:
        A=float(rng.uniform(0.3,0.9)); phase=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
        v=1 + A*np.sin(2*np.pi*x+phase)
    elif kind==3:
        v=rng.uniform(0.2,1.8,m)
    elif kind==4:
        A=float(rng.uniform(0.25,0.75))
        v=1 + A*np.where(np.arange(m)%2==0,1.0,-1.0)
    elif kind==5:
        A=float(rng.uniform(0.3,0.9)); v=1 + A*(2*x-1)
    else:
        v=np.full(m,1.0)
    return np.clip(v,0,2)
```
For m=1, np.arange mod works.

`_perturb_block_vector(v, rng, fraction, sigma)`:
```
v=np.asarray(v,float).copy()
mask=rng.random(v.size)<fraction
if mask.any():
   idx=np.nonzero(mask)[0]
   if rng.random()<0.5: v[idx]=np.clip(v[idx]+rng.normal(0,sigma,idx.size),0,2)
   else: v[idx]=rng.uniform(0,2,idx.size)
return v
```

`_block_candidate(v, rng)`:
```
m=v.size; cand=v.copy(); r=float(rng.random())
if r<0.4:
  idx=int(rng.integers(m)); cand[idx]=np.clip(cand[idx]+float(rng.normal(0,0.2)),0,2)
elif r<0.8:
  w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,m//3)+1))
  idx=np.sort(rng.choice(m,w,replace=False))
  cand[idx]=np.clip(cand[idx]+rng.normal(0,0.2,idx.size),0,2)
else:
  w=int(rng.integers(1,min(3,m)+1))
  idx=rng.choice(m,w,replace=False)
  for i in idx:
     if rng.random()<0.7: cand[i]=np.clip(cand[i]+float(rng.normal(0,0.3)),0,2)
     else: cand[i]=float(rng.choice([0.0,1.0,2.0]))
return cand
```
If m=1, `max(2,m//3)+1` =3, rng.integers(1,3) gives 1 or2 >m? Need `w=int(rng.integers(1, min(m, max(2,m//3)) + 1))`. For m=1, min(1,2)=1, +1=2 => w=1. Good.

`_resample_array(a,n_target)`:
```
a=_normalize_positive(a, a.size if a.size else 1)
if a.size == 0: return np.ones(n_target)
x_old=np.linspace(0,1,a.size)
x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_target)
out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
return _normalize_positive(out,n_target)
```
If a.size=1, interp works.

`_resample_left(l,h_target)`:
```
l=np.clip(np.asarray(l,float),0,2)
if l.size==0: return np.full(h_target,1)
if l.size==h_target: return l
x_old=np.linspace(0,1,l.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,h_target)
out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,l); return np.clip(out,0,2)
```

`_full_left_from_block(v,h_target)`: if h_target % len(v)==0 then repeat else resample left from np.repeat(v, large)? Simpler: `left0 = np.repeat(v, max(1, h_target//max(1,v.size)))` then resample to h_target. Or `np.interp` directly from v. Use interp.
```
def _full_left_from_block(v, h_target):
    v=np.clip(np.asarray(v,float),0,2)
    if v.size==0: return np.full(h_target,1)
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,v.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,h_target)
    return np.clip(np.interp(x_new,x_old,v),0,2)
```
This smooths block steps; if we want exact block repetition, when h_target multiple use repeat.
```
if h_target % v.size ==0: return np.repeat(v, h_target//v.size)
else interp
```
Good.

`_perturb_full(a, rng, fraction, sigma)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float).copy(); n=a.size
mask = rng.random(n) < fraction
if mask.any():
   idx=np.nonzero(mask)[0]
   if rng.random()<0.5:
      a[idx] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,idx.size))
   else:
      a[idx] += rng.normal(0,0.1*sigma+0.01,idx.size)
return _normalize_positive(a,n)
```
If fraction 0, return normalized.

`_log_width(rng,minw,maxw)`:
```
minw=max(1,int(minw)); maxw=max(minw,int(maxw))
if maxw==1: return 1
return int(min(maxw, 1 + np.floor(np.exp(rng.uniform(np.log(minw), np.log(maxw))))))
```
If minw=1, log 0 invalid; use 0 if log1=0 okay. `np.log(1)=0`. Good.

`_subgrad_candidate(left,n,K,rng)`:

Add random to diversify: if rng is not None and c.size > K*2: extra = rng.integers(0, max(1, c.size//10)); if extra not in top (checking small list) append. `np.in1d` small. OK.
- g accumulation.
- If gmax <= eps: `left = np.clip(left + rng.normal(0,0.05,left.size),0,2)` (if rng) else return left.
- scale = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2])))
- lr=scale/gmax; new_left = left - lr*g; clip. If no change, add tiny.
Return.

Need `np.in1d` available. Or skip random extra to avoid bug.

`_full_candidate(a, sigma, rng)` as described. Use `_normalize_positive`. For subgrad, project left, subgrad, rebuild, normalize (anti already sum n but normalize OK). For subgrad, if a size not even? FULL_N even.

`_full_basins(initial_sequences, deadline, rng, max_basins=6, threshold=600)`:
- If no sequences, return None.
- Determine n: use length of sequence with best internal? If inits have different lengths, resample all to length of first or best. We can pass same length. To be safe: compute score for each after resampling to max length? Simpler: `n = initial_sequences[0].size` and resample all to n.
- For each init, `s=_internal_objective(t)`. If inf, skip.
- Sort.
- basins max. If len<max, add perturbed global or random? We can fill with diverse:
   * global best
   * perturbed global fraction 0.1
   * perturbed global fraction 0.3
   * random general
   * anti projection of global (resampled)
But inits already include diverse. Use up to max.
- Stagnation threshold perhaps `threshold = max(200, n//4)`? For n=4096, 1024. Each eval fast; 1000 maybe. Use 500.
- Loop:
   * choose idx: with prob 0.6 the basin with min score (best), else random.
   * candidate = `_full_candidate(basins[idx], 0.1, rng)` (sigma maybe adapt global? Keep 0.1).
   * score. If improved basin, update; if improved global, update global.
   * else stall.
   * if stall > threshold: replace basin:
       - `frac = float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.3))`
       - if rng<0.5: new=_perturb_full(global_best,rng,frac,0.3)
       - elif <0.7: new=_perturb_full(basins[idx],rng,0.5,0.4)
       - else: new=_random_general_array(n,rng)
       - new=_normalize_positive(new,n); score; set basin; if score<global update.
   * print every 20s.
- Return global_best, global_score.

Need if stall replacement creates a basin much worse; that's OK for exploration. But if many basins worse, selection min score picks global best (often basin0). If basin0 stagnates, it gets replaced eventually. Good.

Maybe maintain global_best as separate, and basin with global best can stagnate; replacement frequency ensures new basins.

Potential problem: If no candidate ever improves a basin, it replaces every 500 iterations; time spent on bad basin wasted. But max basins small, ok.

- Full phase:

Prepare full initial sequences:

If full phase returns general best, also compute anti projection and maybe if anti better update global? The global best from full basins may be general. For final, we also want best anti left. We can derive from global best: `best_anti_left = _resample_left(_project_left_from_full(best_full), FULL_N//2)`, `best_anti_a = _anti_from_left(best_anti_left)`, score. Keep in finals.

- Final upscaling:

Let's detail `_block_basins` no lambda:
```
def _block_basins(initial_vectors, repeat, deadline, rng, max_basins=5, threshold=400):
    if len(initial_vectors)==0: return (np.array([]), np.array([]), np.inf)
    vecs=[]; seqs=[]; scores=[]
    for v in initial_vectors:
        v=np.clip(np.asarray(v,dtype=float),0,2)
        a=_block_from_vars(v, repeat)
        s=_internal_objective(a)
        if np.isfinite(s):
           vecs.append(v); seqs.append(a); scores.append(s)
    if len(vecs)==0: return ...
    order=np.argsort(np.array(scores))
    # unique? maybe take first max_basins unique to avoid duplicates
    selected=[]
    for idx in order:
       if len(selected)>=max_basins: break
       # check duplicate? optional
       selected.append(idx)
    basins=[vecs[i].copy() for i in selected]
    basin_scores=[scores[i] for i in selected]
    best_i=selected[0]
    best_v=vecs[best_i].copy(); best_a=seqs[best_i].copy(); best_s=scores[best_i]
    stagnation=[0]*len(basins)
    B=basins[0].size
    next_print=time.monotonic()+10
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if len(basins)==0: break
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
           idx=int(np.argmin(basin_scores))
        else:
           idx=int(rng.integers(len(basins)))
        cand=_block_candidate(basins[idx], rng)
        a_cand=_block_from_vars(cand, repeat)
        s_cand=_internal_objective(a_cand)
        if s_cand < basin_scores[idx]:
           basins[idx]=cand; basin_scores[idx]=s_cand
           if s_cand < best_s:
              best_v=cand.copy(); best_a=a_cand.copy(); best_s=s_cand
           stagnation[idx]=0
        else:
           stagnation[idx]+=1
           if stagnation[idx] > threshold:
              choice=float(rng.random())
              if choice < 0.5:
                 new_v=_perturb_block_vector(best_v, rng, float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.3)), 0.3)
              elif choice < 0.7:
                 new_v=_perturb_block_vector(basins[idx], rng, 0.5, 0.5)
              else:
                 new_v=_initial_block_vector(B, int(rng.integers(0,6)), rng)
              new_v=np.clip(new_v,0,2)
              a_new=_block_from_vars(new_v, repeat)
              s_new=_internal_objective(a_new)
              basins[idx]=new_v; basin_scores[idx]=s_new; stagnation[idx]=0
              if s_new < best_s:
                 best_v=new_v.copy(); best_a=a_new.copy(); best_s=s_new
        if time.monotonic() > next_print:
           print("block basins best %.6f" % best_s)
           next_print=time.monotonic()+15
    return best_v, best_a, best_s
```

```
def _run_block_phase(deadline, rng):
    results=[]
    Bs=[2,4,8,16,32,64]
    # maybe 64 only if time
    remaining=len(Bs)
    for B in Bs:
        rem=deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 5: break
        # if last few and little time, skip large B?
        per=max(5, rem / max(1, remaining))
        local_deadline=time.monotonic() + per
        repeat=max(1, 256 // B)
        inits=[_initial_block_vector(B, kind, rng) for kind in range(6)]
        # maybe if B=64 and time small, only 3 kinds
        v,a,s=_block_basins(inits, repeat, local_deadline, rng, max_basins=4, threshold=300)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            results.append((B,v,a,s))
            print("B",B,"score %.6f"%s)
        remaining-=1
    return results
```

`_lp_optimize_sequence` print. Need if `_HAVE_LINPROG` false, just return internal score.

```
def _lp_optimize_sequence(a, deadline, rng):
    a=_normalize_positive(a, a.size)
    n=a.size
    best_a=a.copy(); best_s=_internal_objective(a)
    if not _HAVE_LINPROG or n < 16: return best_a,best_s
    a=best_a.copy()
    no_improve=0
    M_factor=1.0
    next_print=time.monotonic()+20
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
       c=_fft_convolve(a)
       if c.size==0: break
       M=float(c.max())
       if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0: break
       b=_lp_direction(a, M*M_factor)
       if b is None:
           if M_factor > 0.7:
               M_factor -= 0.05
           else:
               break
           continue
       sum_b=float(np.sum(b))
       if sum_b <= 1e-10:
           M_factor += 0.05
           M_factor = min(1.0, M_factor)
           continue
       g=b * (float(np.sum(a)) / sum_b)
       g=_normalize_positive(g, n)
       best_cand=a; best_cand_s=best_s
       for t in (0.5, 0.35, 0.25, 0.15, 0.08, 0.04, 0.02):
           cand=(1.0-t)*a + t*g
           cand=_normalize_positive(cand, n)
           s=_internal_objective(cand)
           if s < best_cand_s:
               best_cand=cand; best_cand_s=s
       if best_cand_s < best_s - 1e-10:
           a=best_cand.copy(); best_a=a.copy(); best_s=best_cand_s; no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve >= 2:
               M_factor -= 0.05
               if M_factor < 0.7:
                   break
           elif no_improve > 20:
               break
       if time.monotonic() > next_print:
           print("lp n=%d score %.6f factor %.2f" % (n,best_s,M_factor))
           next_print=time.monotonic()+30
    return best_a, best_s
```

if no_improve > 0 and no_improve % 3 ==0: M_factor=max(0.7,M_factor-0.05)
if no_improve > 15: break
```

`_lp_direction` if n>1200 return None to avoid memory. Use n_lp ≤1024. If `M <=0` return zeros? None.

```
def _lp_direction(a, M):
    if not _HAVE_LINPROG: return None
    n=a.size
    if n < 8 or n > 1200: return None
    if M <= 0 or not np.isfinite(M): return None
    C=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        C[j:j+n, j] = a
    c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=C, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
       if res.success and res.x is not None:
          x=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
          x=np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
          x=np.clip(x,0,1000)
          if float(np.sum(x)) > 1e-12:
             return x
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Memory: `bounds` list of 1200 tuples OK. `linprog` may take time. Good.

`_subgrad_candidate` implementation:
```
def _subgrad_candidate(left, n, K, rng):
    left=np.clip(np.asarray(left,dtype=float),0,2)
    h=n//2
    if h <=0: return left
    a=_anti_from_left(left)
    c=_fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size ==0: return left
    K=int(min(max(1,K), c.size-1))
    top=np.argsort(c)[-K:]
    if rng is not None:
        # add random from top quarter maybe
        third=max(1, c.size//4)
        extra=int(rng.integers(0, third))
        # small list check
        found=False
        for idx in top:
           if int(idx)==int(extra): found=True; break
        if not found:
           top=np.append(top, extra)
    g=np.zeros(h, dtype=float)
    for k in top:
       k=int(k)
       lo=max(0, k-(n-1))
       hi=min(h-1, k)
       if hi >= lo:
           jmax=k-lo
           jmin=k-hi
           # if jmax >= jmin
           segment = a[jmax:jmin-1:-1]
           g[lo:hi+1] += segment
       lo2=max(0, n-1-k)
       hi2=min(h-1, 2*n-2-k)
       if hi2 >= lo2:
           jmin2=k-n+1+lo2
           jmax2=k-n+1+hi2
           g[lo2:hi2+1] -= a[jmin2:jmax2+1]
    g *= 2.0
    gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if gmax <= 1e-12:
       if rng is not None:
          left = np.clip(left + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, h), 0.0, 2.0)
       return left
    scale_options=np.array([0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2])
    scale=float(rng.choice(scale_options)) if rng is not None else 0.05
    lr=scale/gmax
    new_left=left - lr*g
    new_left=np.clip(new_left,0,2)
    if np.all(new_left == left):
       if rng is not None:
          new_left=np.clip(left + rng.normal(0.0,0.02,h),0,2)
    return new_left
```
Check slice `a[jmax:jmin-1:-1]`: if jmin=0, jmin-1=-1, slice a[jmax:-1:-1] gives down to index0 exclusive -1? In Python, `a[3:-1:-1]` returns [a3]? Let's test mentally: stop -1 means index len-1, step -1, start 3, stops before -1 (which is last index), so only a3 if start=3. Good. If jmax>jmin, works. If jmax==jmin, `a[jmax:jmax-1:-1]` returns element jmax. OK. If segmented length matches hi-lo+1. Good.
Negative slice `a[jmin2:jmax2+1]`. Good.

`_full_candidate`:
```
def _full_candidate(a, sigma, rng):
    n=a.size
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    r=float(rng.random())
    if r < 0.30:
       left=_project_left_from_full(a)
       left=_subgrad_candidate(left, n, int(int(rng.integers(4,17))), rng)
       return _anti_from_left(left)
    cand=a.copy()
    if r < 0.60:
       maxw=max(4, n//64)
       w=_log_width(1, maxw, rng)
       w=min(w,n)
       s=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
           cand[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), w))
       else:
           cand[s:s+w] += rng.normal(0.0, 0.05 + 0.1*float(sigma), w)
    elif r < 0.80:
       maxw=max(2, n//256)
       w=int(rng.integers(1, maxw+1))
       w=min(w,n)
       s=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       base=np.clip(cand[s:s+w],0,None)
       if float(rng.random()) < 0.6:
           cand[s:s+w] = np.clip(base + rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), w), 0.0, None)
       else:
           mean=float(base.mean()) if base.size>0 else 1.0
           cand[s:s+w] = np.clip(rng.normal(mean, 0.2, w), 0.0, None)
    elif r < 0.90:
       maxw=max(3, n//128)
       w=int(rng.integers(1, maxw+1))
       s=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       mean=float(cand[s:s+w].mean()) if w>0 else 1.0
       cand[s:s+w] = rng.uniform(0.0, max(0.2, mean), w)
    else:
       if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
           cand = 0.5*(a + a[::-1])
       else:
           left=_project_left_from_full(a)
           left=np.clip(left + rng.normal(0.0,0.05,left.size),0,2)
           cand=_anti_from_left(left)
    return _normalize_positive(cand, n)
```
Use `float(sigma)` where needed. `int(rng.integers(4,17))` returns int64, fine.

`_log_width`:
```
def _log_width(rng, minw, maxw):
    minw=int(max(1,minw)); maxw=int(max(minw,maxw))
    if maxw <=1: return 1
    val=float(np.exp(rng.uniform(float(np.log(minw)), float(np.log(maxw)))))
    return int(min(maxw, max(1, 1 + int(np.floor(val)))))
```
If minw=1, log=0; exp uniform [0, log(max)] -> [1,max]. floor+1 in [1,max+1] cap. Good.

`_full_basins` no lambda:
```
def _full_basins(initial_sequences, deadline, rng, max_basins=8, threshold=600):
    if len(initial_sequences)==0: return None, np.inf
    # choose n as length of first finite
    base_n=initial_sequences[0].size
    processed=[]; scores=[]
    for seq in initial_sequences:
       s=_resample_array(seq, base_n)
       s=_normalize_positive(s, base_n)
       sc=_internal_objective(s)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
          processed.append(s); scores.append(sc)
    if len(processed)==0: return None,np.inf
    order=np.argsort(np.array(scores))
    selected=[]
    for idx in order:
       if len(selected)>=max_basins: break
       selected.append(idx)
    basins=[processed[i].copy() for i in selected]
    basin_scores=[scores[i] for i in selected]
    best_idx=selected[0]
    best_a=processed[best_idx].copy(); best_s=scores[best_idx]
    stagnation=[0]*len(basins)
    sigma=0.1
    next_print=time.monotonic()+15
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if len(basins)==0: break
       if float(rng.random()) < 0.6:
           idx=int(np.argmin(basin_scores))
       else:
           idx=int(rng.integers(len(basins)))
       cand=_full_candidate(basins[idx], sigma, rng)
       sc=_internal_objective(cand)
       if sc < basin_scores[idx]:
          basins[idx]=cand; basin_scores[idx]=sc
          if sc < best_s:
             best_a=cand.copy(); best_s=sc
          stagnation[idx]=0
       else:
          stagnation[idx]+=1
          if stagnation[idx] > threshold:
             choice=float(rng.random())
             frac=float(rng.uniform(0.03,0.25))
             if choice < 0.45:
                new_a=_perturb_full(best_a, rng, frac, 0.3)
             elif choice < 0.7:
                new_a=_perturb_full(basins[idx], rng, 0.5, 0.4)
             else:
                new_a=_random_general_array(base_n, rng)
             new_sc=_internal_objective(new_a)
             basins[idx]=new_a; basin_scores[idx]=new_sc; stagnation[idx]=0
             if new_sc < best_s:
                best_a=new_a.copy(); best_s=new_sc
       # adapt sigma? if best improved? keep
       if time.monotonic() > next_print:
          print("full basins best %.6f" % best_s)
          next_print=time.monotonic()+20
    return best_a, best_s
```
If processed len < max, OK. If `base_n` small? FULL_N at least 2048.

`_random_general_array(n, rng)`:
```
def _random_general_array(n, rng):
    a=rng.normal(1.0,0.3,n)
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    if a.sum() <= 0:
       a=np.ones(n)
    return _normalize_positive(a,n)
```

Need careful with time deadlines:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    total=int(max(10, float(budget_s)-5))
    deadline=start+total
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    parent=None
    pv = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    try: ...
    except: pass
    # remaining helper function? no closures, just compute.
    block_budget = float(min(90.0, total * 0.06))
    block_deadline = min(deadline - 300.0, start + block_budget)
    if block_deadline < start + 10: block_deadline = start + min(20, total*0.05)
    block_results=_run_block_phase(block_deadline, rng)
    # lp
    best_lp_a=None; best_lp_s=np.inf
    if _HAVE_LINPROG:
       n_lp=512
       lp_starts=[]
       # top block results sorted by score using argsort
       if block_results:
          br_scores=np.array([s for _,_,_,s in block_results])
          order=np.argsort(br_scores)
          for idx in order:
             B,v,a,s=block_results[idx]
             left=_full_left_from_block(v, n_lp//2)
             arr=_anti_from_left(left)
             arr=_normalize_positive(arr,n_lp)
             lp_starts.append(arr)
             if len(lp_starts)>=3: break
       # add defaults
       h=n_lp//2
       left=np.full(h, 4.0/3.0)
       lp_starts.append(_anti_from_left(left))
       x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,h)
       left=np.clip(1.0 + 0.6*np.sin(2*np.pi*x),0.0,2.0)
       lp_starts.append(_anti_from_left(left))
       left=np.clip(rng.uniform(0.2,1.8,h),0,2)
       lp_starts.append(_anti_from_left(left))
       if parent is not None and parent.size >= 100:
          lp_starts.append(_resample_array(parent,n_lp))
       # limit 4
       lp_starts=lp_starts[:4]
       rem_arr = [(n_lp, a) for n_lp,a in ...]
       remaining = len(lp_starts)
       for idx,a in enumerate(lp_starts):
          rem=deadline - time.monotonic()
          lp_dead = min(deadline - 240.0, time.monotonic() + max(20.0, rem / max(1, remaining)))
          if time.monotonic() < lp_dead - 20:
             a_new,s_new=_lp_optimize_sequence(a, lp_dead, rng)
             if s_new < best_lp_s:
                best_lp_a=a_new; best_lp_s=s_new
          remaining -=1
       # optional second LP at 1024
       rem=deadline - time.monotonic()
       if best_lp_a is not None and rem > 600:
          a2=_resample_array(best_lp_a, 1024)
          lp2_dead = min(deadline - 300.0, time.monotonic() + min(300.0, rem*0.3))
          if lp2_dead > time.monotonic() + 60:
             a2,s2=_lp_optimize_sequence(a2, lp2_dead, rng)
             if s2 < best_lp_s:
                best_lp_a=a2; best_lp_s=s2
    # If LP failed, use best block result
    if best_lp_a is None:
       if block_results:
          br_scores=np.array([s for _,_,_,s in block_results])
          idx=int(np.argmin(br_scores))
          B,v,a,s=block_results[idx]
          # maybe resample to 1024
          best_lp_a=_resample_array(a, 1024)
          best_lp_s=_internal_objective(best_lp_a)
       else:
          best_lp_a=_random_general_array(512,rng)
          best_lp_s=_internal_objective(best_lp_a)
    # full initial
    if float(budget_s) > 1200:
       FULL_N=4096
    else:
       FULL_N=2048
    h_full=FULL_N//2
    full_starts=[]
    full_starts.append(_resample_array(best_lp_a, FULL_N))
    left_proj=_project_left_from_full(best_lp_a)
    left_res=_resample_left(left_proj, h_full)
    full_starts.append(_normalize_positive(_anti_from_left(left_res), FULL_N))
    if block_results:
       br_scores=np.array([s for _,_,_,s in block_results])
       order=np.argsort(br_scores)
       count=0
       for idx in order:
          B,v,a,s=block_results[idx]
          left=_full_left_from_block(v, h_full)
          arr=_anti_from_left(left)
          full_starts.append(_normalize_positive(arr,FULL_N))
          count+=1
          if count>=3: break
    # defaults
    left=np.full(h_full, 4.0/3.0)
    full_starts.append(_anti_from_left(left))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,h_full)
    left=np.clip(1.0+0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*x),0,2)
    full_starts.append(_anti_from_left(left))
    if parent is not None and parent.size>=100:
       full_starts.append(_resample_array(parent,FULL_N))
    full_deadline = deadline - 60
    best_full,best_full_s=_full_basins(full_starts, full_deadline, rng, max_basins=8, threshold=800)
    if best_full is None:
       best_full=best_lp_a; best_full_s=best_lp_s
    # final candidates
    best_anti_left = _resample_left(_project_left_from_full(best_full), FULL_N//2)
    best_anti_a = _anti_from_left(best_anti_left)
    final_candidates=[]
    targets=set()
    targets.add(FULL_N)
    if 2*FULL_N <= 10000: targets.add(2*FULL_N)
    targets.add(8192)
    if parent is not None:
       pl=int(parent.size)
       if pl %2 ==1: pl-=1
       if 800 <= pl <= 10000: targets.add(pl)
    for t in targets:
       if t < 64: continue
       ag=_resample_array(best_full, int(t))
       sg=_internal_objective(ag)
       if np.isfinite(sg): final_candidates.append((ag,sg))
       h_t=int(t)//2
       if h_t >0:
          la=_resample_left(best_anti_left, h_t)  # maybe resample from full anti left size FULL_N/2
          aa=_anti_from_left(la)
          sa=_internal_objective(aa)
          if np.isfinite(sa): final_candidates.append((aa,sa))
    if not final_candidates:
       final_seq=_sanitize_sequence(best_full)
    else:
       scores_arr=np.array([s for a,s in final_candidates])
       order=np.argsort(scores_arr)
       final_seq=final_candidates[int(order[0])][0]
    # direct evaluate top
    best_true_score=np.inf; best_true_seq=final_seq
    if len(final_candidates)>0:
       scores_arr=np.array([s for a,s in final_candidates])
       order=np.argsort(scores_arr)
       count=0
       for idx in order:
          if time.monotonic() > deadline - 12: break
          if count >= 4: break
          a_candidate=final_candidates[int(idx)][0]
          internal_score=final_candidates[int(idx)][1]
          # skip if internal much worse than current best_true? But we don't have best_true until eval. Use first 3.
          try:
             ts=evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in a_candidate])
             ts=float(ts)
             if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < best_true_score:
                 best_true_score=ts; best_true_seq=a_candidate
          except Exception:
             pass
          count+=1
    final_seq = best_true_seq if best_true_score < np.inf else final_seq
    final_seq = _sanitize_sequence(final_seq)
    # If final length too large? ensure <20000
    if final_seq.size > 12000:
       final_seq=_resample_array(final_seq, 8192)
    return [float(x) for x in final_seq]
```
Need `_sanitize_sequence` defined; we have `_normalize_positive` but final seq may be general, use `_normalize_positive(seq, seq.size)`. We can define `_sanitize_sequence` as alias.

`_subgrad_candidate`: `top = np.argsort(c)[-K:]`; if K = c.size-1, top all but lowest. Good. `extra = int(rng.integers(0, third))` where third = max(1,c.size//4). This random from low quarter? For c size 4095, third 1023, indices 0..1022 (lower lags). Maybe should random from top quarter? Doesn't matter. Could choose from all maybe `c.size`. Could skip random extra to keep strong subgrad. But diversity maybe. In full basins, random candidates provide diversity. Let's remove random extra for simplicity/performance. Or add with probability 0.2 from all indices? Not needed. Simpler no random extra.

Good.
